Critical

CISA KEV: CVE-2026-10520 — Ivanti Sentry OS Command Injection Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-06-11 1 use case 1 technique 2 kill-chain phases detected

Ivanti Sentry (formerly known as MobileIron Sentry) contains an OS command injection vulnerability which could allow a remote unauthenticated user to achieve root-level remote code execution. This vulnerability can be successfully exploited in cases where the Sentry appliance is in an unmanaged state with its endpoints externally reachable. The use of mTLS with EPMM or restricted HTTPS access through Neurons for MDM makes interfaces inaccessible to external actors. Vendor: Ivanti, Product: Sentry. Federal patch due: 2026-06-14.

Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-10520", "CVE-2026-10523")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-10520", "CVE-2026-10523")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("2553649f2322049666871cea80a5d0d6adc700ca", "d6f3f62fd3b9f5432f5782b62d8cfd5247d5ee71", "07d889e2dadce6f3910dcbc253317d28ca61c766") or SHA1 in~ ("2553649f2322049666871cea80a5d0d6adc700ca", "d6f3f62fd3b9f5432f5782b62d8cfd5247d5ee71", "07d889e2dadce6f3910dcbc253317d28ca61c766") or MD5 in~ ("2553649f2322049666871cea80a5d0d6adc700ca", "d6f3f62fd3b9f5432f5782b62d8cfd5247d5ee71", "07d889e2dadce6f3910dcbc253317d28ca61c766")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("2553649f2322049666871cea80a5d0d6adc700ca", "d6f3f62fd3b9f5432f5782b62d8cfd5247d5ee71", "07d889e2dadce6f3910dcbc253317d28ca61c766") or SHA1 in~ ("2553649f2322049666871cea80a5d0d6adc700ca", "d6f3f62fd3b9f5432f5782b62d8cfd5247d5ee71", "07d889e2dadce6f3910dcbc253317d28ca61c766") or MD5 in~ ("2553649f2322049666871cea80a5d0d6adc700ca", "d6f3f62fd3b9f5432f5782b62d8cfd5247d5ee71", "07d889e2dadce6f3910dcbc253317d28ca61c766")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["2553649f2322049666871cea80a5d0d6adc700ca", "d6f3f62fd3b9f5432f5782b62d8cfd5247d5ee71", "07d889e2dadce6f3910dcbc253317d28ca61c766"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → Suspicious browser extension installation
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs
Suspicious browser extension installation Installation Medium
Side-loaded / unsigned extensions, often masquerading as wallets, productivity tools.

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"

| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes

      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

    by Vulnerabilities.dest, Vulnerabilities.signature, Vulnerabilities.severity, Vulnerabilities.cve

| sort - severity

ATT&CKT1190 T1566

Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution · Email attachment opened from external sender
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Office app spawning script/LOLBin child process · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")

                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")

     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time

Email attachment opened from external sender Delivery High
Attachment delivery + execution by recipient — most common malware initial access.
ATT&CKT1566.001 T1204.002
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Endpoint.Processes EmailAttachmentInfo DeviceProcessEvents
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MalAttachments = EmailAttachmentInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project NetworkMessageId, RecipientEmailAddress,
              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("OUTLOOK.EXE","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                      "mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe")
| join kind=inner MalAttachments on $left.AccountUpn == $right.RecipientEmailAddress
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, AttachmentFileName, AttachmentSHA256
// Sentinel doesn't have an attachment-hash table. Best approximation:
// OfficeActivity Send/Receive activity + correlate to host process spawn.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MailWithAttach = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation in~ ("Send","Receive")
    | where isnotempty(MessageId)
    | project EmailTime = TimeGenerated, MailboxOwnerUPN, Sender,
              Recipients, Subject, MessageId;
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith "OUTLOOK.EXE"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Office Application Spawning Script Host
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d07
status: experimental
description: |
    Word/Excel/Outlook/OneNote spawning powershell, cmd, wscript,
    cscript, mshta, rundll32, regsvr32 — classic macro / embedded-
    object payload signature.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1566/001/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059
    - attack.t1566.001
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_parent:
        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\winword.exe'
            - '\excel.exe'
            - '\powerpnt.exe'
            - '\outlook.exe'
            - '\onenote.exe'
            - '\mspub.exe'
            - '\visio.exe'
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
    condition: selection_parent and selection_child
falsepositives:
    - Legitimate automation macros in trusted internal documents
level: high
ENDPOINT

ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic

Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents

| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1021.002
    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist
level: high

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```

    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url

| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha) Exploitation High

ATT&CKT1204.004 T1059.001

DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("explorer.exe","RuntimeBroker.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","mshta.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(iex|invoke-expression|frombase64|downloadstring|hxxp|curl |wget )"

   or ParentProcessName endswith "\RuntimeBroker.exe"

   or NewProcessName endswith "\pwsh.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"

title: FakeCaptcha / ClickFix - Clipboard-Injected PowerShell from Explorer
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d06
status: experimental
description: |
    "FakeCaptcha" / "ClickFix" social-engineering: malicious site asks
    user to press Win+R and paste a payload. The result is
    powershell/mshta launched directly from explorer.exe with iex /
    base64 / downloadstring in the command line.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1204/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1204.002
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_parent:
        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\explorer.exe'
            - '\RuntimeBroker.exe'
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
    selection_cmd:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - 'iex'
            - 'invoke-expression'
            - 'frombase64'
            - 'downloadstring'
            - 'hxxp'
            - 'curl '
            - 'wget '
    condition: selection_parent and selection_child and selection_cmd
falsepositives:
    - IT-driven scripted automation that admins paste-launch
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","mshta.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*iex*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*hxxp*" OR Processes.process="*curl*" OR Processes.process="*wget*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","mshta.exe")

Miasma Worm Hits Microsoft Again: Azure Functions Action and 72 Other Repositories Disabled After Supply Chain Attack Targeting AI Coding Agents

StepSecurity
2026-06-09 12 use cases 4 techniques 5 kill-chain phases detected

On June 5, 2026, the Miasma worm campaign reached Microsoft's Azure GitHub organizations. GitHub disabled 73 repositories across four Microsoft GitHub organizations after a malicious commit was pushed to the Azure/durabletask repository using a previously compromised contributor account. The attack planted configuration files that execute a credential-harvesting payload when a developer opens the repository in Claude Code, Gemini CLI, Cursor, or VS Code.

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] Suspicious commit pattern: '[skip ci]' with backdated timestamp adding only IDE config files · Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] node.exe spawned by Code/Cursor/Claude/Gemini executing .github/setup.js · Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha) · PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Miasma Worm Hits Microsoft Again: Azure Functions Action and 72 Other Repositori
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Malicious AI coding-agent hook configs written to repo (.claude/.gemini/.cursor/.vscode) · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Outbound DNS / HTTP to Miasma C2 (git-service.com / m-kosche.com) · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Malicious AI coding-agent hook configs written to repo (.claude/.gemini/.cursor/.vscode) AIInstallation High
Hunts the Miasma worm's signature artifact: simultaneous creation of SessionStart hooks and folderOpen tasks in .claude/settings.json, .gemini/settings.json, .cursor/rules/setup.mdc, and .vscode/tasks.json — all pointing at node .github/setup.js. This is the precise file-set planted in the June 5 2026 Azure/durabletask commit 5f456b8. Rationale: The five planted-file paths are verbatim from the article's 'Five Planted Files, Four Attack Vectors' section. Requiring >=2 co-occurrence per host suppresses single legitimate .vscode/tasks.json edits while keeping near-zero FP on the Miasma file-set. Cross-checked against: • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/002/ • https://github.com/step-security/supply-chain-firewall
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1546
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Developer scaffolding a new repo with VS Code tasks.json AND committing a .vscode workspace file at the same time
  • Engineering platform team rolling out a corporate .cursor/rules/ template across repos
  • Repo containing legitimate .github/setup.js for project bootstrapping (rare; size + obfuscation differentiates)
  • Renovate/Dependabot edits to .vscode/tasks.json without other paired files (will not trigger >=2 threshold)
  • Developer copying a template repo locally that legitimately includes both .claude and .vscode dirs
Suppression filter
| where not (InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("git.exe","Code.exe","Cursor.exe") and array_length(ArtifactsSeen) == 1 and ArtifactsSeen has "vscode-folderopen")
Response runbook
Step 1 — Triage: pull the InitiatingProcess (typically git.exe / Code.exe checkout) and inspect the .github/setup.js size and entropy; the Miasma payload is exactly 4,643,745 bytes single-line obfuscated JS. Pivot to DeviceProcessEvents for any node.exe child within 60s. Step 2 — Contain: isolate the host, kill any node setup.js process tree, force-revoke the developer's GitHub PAT / OAuth tokens, cloud CLI tokens (aws, az, gcloud), and rotate any .npmrc / .pypirc / kubeconfig secrets that the harvester targets. Step 3 — Hunt: search the org for any repo containing the five planted paths via GitHub code-search API; check for outbound DNS to check.git-service.com / t.m-kosche.com / git-service.com from any developer endpoint in the past 30 days.
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where FolderPath has_any (@"\.claude\settings.json", @"\.gemini\settings.json", @"\.cursor\rules\setup.mdc", @"\.vscode\tasks.json", @"\.github\setup.js")
| extend Artifact = case(
    FolderPath has @"\.claude\settings.json", "claude-hook",
    FolderPath has @"\.gemini\settings.json", "gemini-hook",
    FolderPath has @"\.cursor\rules\setup.mdc", "cursor-prompt-injection",
    FolderPath has @"\.vscode\tasks.json", "vscode-folderopen",
    FolderPath has @"\.github\setup.js", "miasma-payload", "other")
| summarize ArtifactsSeen = make_set(Artifact), Paths = make_set(FolderPath), FirstSeen = min(Timestamp) by DeviceId, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| where array_length(ArtifactsSeen) >= 2  // co-occurrence of >=2 of the 5 planted files
| order by FirstSeen desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileCreate")
| where TargetFilePath has_any (@"\.claude\settings.json", @"\.gemini\settings.json", @"\.cursor\rules\setup.mdc", @"\.vscode\tasks.json", @"\.github\setup.js")
| extend Artifact = case(
    TargetFilePath has @"\.claude\settings.json", "claude-hook",
    TargetFilePath has @"\.gemini\settings.json", "gemini-hook",
    TargetFilePath has @"\.cursor\rules\setup.mdc", "cursor-prompt-injection",
    TargetFilePath has @"\.vscode\tasks.json", "vscode-folderopen",
    TargetFilePath has @"\.github\setup.js", "miasma-payload", "other")
| summarize ArtifactsSeen = make_set(Artifact), Paths = make_set(TargetFilePath), FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated) by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName
| where array_length(ArtifactsSeen) >= 2
| order by FirstSeen desc

node.exe spawned by Code/Cursor/Claude/Gemini executing .github/setup.js AIExploitation High
Hunts the Miasma worm's automatic execution chain: VS Code, Cursor, Claude Code, or Gemini CLI launching node.exe with .github/setup.js as the script argument. This is the runtime detonation produced when a developer simply opens a repo containing the planted hook configs. Rationale: Article quotes the exact command 'node .github/setup.js' invoked by all four planted configs (Claude SessionStart, Gemini SessionStart, Cursor prompt injection, VS Code folderOpen task). Anchoring on parent IDE + node.exe + setup.js path is high-fidelity because legitimate projects rarely store bootstrap scripts under .github/. Cross-checked against: • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1546/ • https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/editor/tasks#_run-behavior
ATT&CKT1059.007 T1546 T1204.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Developer with a legitimate project-bootstrap node script genuinely under .github/setup.js
  • Internal devtools team running .github/setup.js via VS Code task as a sanctioned scaffold
  • CI runner image with cached IDE binaries executing setup scripts during build
  • Repo template repos where setup.js is an intentional installer
  • Security researcher analysing the Miasma sample in a sandbox (whitelist sandbox host IDs)
Suppression filter
| where not (DeviceName has "sandbox" or DeviceName has "malware-lab")
Response runbook
Step 1 — Triage: hash the setup.js (expect 4,643,745 bytes / SHA256 mismatch to any known-good repo) and capture the first 1 KB of the single-line obfuscated JS for IR. Pull network egress from the node.exe PID. Step 2 — Contain: kill the node process tree, isolate the host, and revoke developer-context credentials — GitHub PAT/OAuth, AWS/Azure/GCP CLI tokens, kubeconfig, NPM/PyPI tokens, SSH keys (article confirms harvester targets AWS/Azure/GCP/Kubernetes + 90+ dev tool configs). Step 3 — Hunt: enumerate all repos cloned on the host in the past 30 days and check each working tree for the five planted paths; sweep the org's GitHub for commits with message 'Switched DataConverter to OrchestrationContext [skip ci]' or backdated commits to 2020-03-09.

| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("Code.exe","Cursor.exe","claude.exe","claude-code.exe","gemini.exe","node.exe","powershell.exe","cmd.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildImage = FolderPath,
          ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine,
          SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc

Outbound DNS / HTTP to Miasma C2 (git-service.com / m-kosche.com) AICommand & Control High
Hunts resolution or connection to the Miasma worm's credential-exfiltration infrastructure: check.git-service.com, git-service.com, and t.m-kosche.com. These domains receive harvested AWS/Azure/GCP/Kubernetes/dev-tool secrets from the planted payload. Rationale: Three domains are explicitly listed in the article's pre-extracted IOCs (check.git-service.com, t.m-kosche.com, git-service.com). Article says credential-harvesting payload exfiltrates AWS/Azure/GCP/Kubernetes/dev-tool secrets, so DNS+network egress to these domains is high-fidelity C2. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/miasma-worm-hits-microsoft-again-azure-functions-action-and-72-other-repositories-disabled-after-supply-chain-attack-targeting-ai-coding-agents

Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Network_Resolution.DNS
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Legitimate enterprise self-hosted Git on a sub-domain that coincidentally matches *git-service.com (rare; whitelist by exact FQDN)
  • Threat-intel feed fetcher / sandbox detonation system (whitelist sandbox host IDs)
  • Security researcher visiting the domain for reverse-engineering
  • DNS sinkhole or RPZ infrastructure resolving the indicator (whitelist sinkhole resolver IP)
  • Browsing to the StepSecurity blog post itself (won't hit subdomains directly)
Suppression filter

Response runbook
Step 1 — Triage: identify the initiating process and user; if InitiatingProcessFileName is node.exe, immediately pivot to its command line for .github/setup.js. Step 2 — Contain: isolate the host, block the three domains at the corporate resolver and egress proxy, and treat all credentials accessible from the user/host context as compromised — rotate cloud tokens, GitHub PATs, NPM/PyPI/Cargo registry tokens, kubeconfig, SSH keys. Step 3 — Hunt: 30-day lookback across the entire fleet for any resolution of git-service.com or m-kosche.com; correlate hits with file-creation of .github/setup.js or the four IDE hook configs to find latent infections.

Suspicious commit pattern: '[skip ci]' with backdated timestamp adding only IDE config files AIDelivery Medium
Hunts the Miasma commit fingerprint: a single push where 5 files added / 0 source files modified, commit message ends with '[skip ci]', author date backdated to a pre-2024 timestamp, and the added paths include AI-agent config dirs. Detects via GitHub Audit Log shipped to Sentinel/Defender via CloudAppEvents or to Datadog via the GitHub integration. Rationale: Article details the exact commit fingerprint: commit 5f456b8 with message 'Switched DataConverter to OrchestrationContext [skip ci]', 5 files added / 0 source files modified, backdated to 2020-03-09T15:59:47Z (six years before push). The conjunction of [skip ci] + backdated author date + added IDE-config paths is the Miasma operator signature. Cross-checked against: • https://docs.github.com/en/enterprise-cloud@latest/admin/monitoring-activity-in-your-enterprise/reviewing-audit-logs-for-your-enterprise
ATT&CKT1195.001 T1078.004
Data sourcesChange.All_Changes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Legitimate maintainer importing an old Git history with [skip ci] in a merge commit
  • Repository migration from another VCS preserving original author dates pre-2024
  • CI maintenance commit adding .vscode/tasks.json with [skip ci] (will not match >=2 of the IDE paths usually)
  • Bot commits (Renovate/Dependabot) with skip-ci that touch tasks.json
  • Developer rebasing old branches with preserved author timestamps
Response runbook
Step 1 — Triage: pull the actor account from the audit event, check its recent activity for token regeneration / SSH key additions / new OAuth grants; correlate against the May 19 PyPI durabletask incident actor. Step 2 — Contain: suspend the GitHub account, revoke all PATs and OAuth grants, force-rotate any GitHub App / Action secrets the account had access to, and revert the commit from any affected default branches. Step 3 — Hunt: GitHub code-search the org for any other repos containing the five planted paths or the commit message 'Switched DataConverter to OrchestrationContext'; query GitHub Audit Log for the same actor across all owned orgs for [skip ci] commits adding files outside src trees.
CloudAppEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where Application == "GitHub"
| where ActionType in ("Repositories.Push","RepositoriesPush","git.push")
| extend Raw = tostring(RawEventData)
| where Raw has "[skip ci]"
| where Raw has_any (".claude/settings.json",".gemini/settings.json",".cursor/rules/setup.mdc",".vscode/tasks.json",".github/setup.js")
| extend AuthorDate = extract(@"author[^\d]+(\d{4})-\d{2}-\d{2}", 1, Raw)
| where isnotempty(AuthorDate) and toint(AuthorDate) < 2024
| project Timestamp, AccountDisplayName, AccountObjectId, IPAddress, ObjectName, AuthorDate, ActionType
| order by Timestamp desc
// Requires GitHub Audit Log connector (GitHubAuditLogPolling_CL) or CommonSecurityLog ingest
GitHubAuditLogPolling_CL
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where action_s == "git.push" or action_s == "repo.push"
| where commits_s has "[skip ci]"
| where commits_s has_any (".claude/settings.json",".gemini/settings.json",".cursor/rules/setup.mdc",".vscode/tasks.json",".github/setup.js")
| extend AuthorYear = extract(@"author[^\d]+(\d{4})-\d{2}-\d{2}", 1, commits_s)
| where isnotempty(AuthorYear) and toint(AuthorYear) < 2024
| project TimeGenerated, actor_s, actor_ip_s, repo_s, commits_s, AuthorYear
| order by TimeGenerated desc
source:github @evt.name:git.push @commits.message:*"[skip ci]"* (@commits.added:(*.claude/settings.json* OR *.gemini/settings.json* OR *.cursor/rules/setup.mdc* OR *.vscode/tasks.json* OR *.github/setup.js*))
CLOUD
`github_audit` action=git.push | spath input=raw_event | search commits{}.message="*[skip ci]*" (commits{}.added{}="*.claude/settings.json" OR commits{}.added{}="*.gemini/settings.json" OR commits{}.added{}="*.cursor/rules/setup.mdc" OR commits{}.added{}="*.vscode/tasks.json" OR commits{}.added{}="*.github/setup.js") | eval author_year=substr('commits{}.timestamp',1,4) | where author_year<"2024" | table _time repo actor commits{}.id commits{}.message commits{}.added{} author_year
Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic

    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

title: FakeCaptcha / ClickFix - Clipboard-Injected PowerShell from Explorer
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d06
status: experimental
description: |
    "FakeCaptcha" / "ClickFix" social-engineering: malicious site asks
    user to press Win+R and paste a payload. The result is
    powershell/mshta launched directly from explorer.exe with iex /
    base64 / downloadstring in the command line.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/

    - attack.t1059.001

    category: process_creation
    product: windows

        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\explorer.exe'
            - '\RuntimeBroker.exe'

            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'

            - 'invoke-expression'
            - 'frombase64'

            - 'wget '

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02

    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access

    category: process_creation
    product: windows

        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'

            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["069ac1dc7f7649b76bc72a11ac700f373804bfd81dab7e561157b703999f44ce", "3de04fe2a76262743ed089efa7115f4508619838e77d60b9a1aab8b20d2cc8bf", "85f54c089d78ebfb101454ec934c767065a342a43c9ee1beac8430cdd3b2086f", "c0b094e46842260936d4b97ce63e4539b99a3eae48b736798c700217c52569dc", "3a9db5ba0c8cd4c91e91717df6b1a141fc1e0fbc0558b5a78d7f5c23f5b2a150", "d630397de8b01af0f6f5cf4463da91b17f28195a2c50c8f3f38ad9f7873fdb8e", "633c8410ee0413ca4b090a19c30b20c03f31598c25247c484846fa34c1df5b64", "ef641e956f91d501b748085996303c96a64d67f63bfeef0dda175e5aa19cca90"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] GlobalProtect successful login from CVE-2026-0257 pre-PoC exploitation IPs · [LLM] GlobalProtect connection with PoC-spoofed host-ID, MAC, or device name · [LLM] GlobalProtect connection with PoC client fingerprint: Win10 Pro 64-bit + empty d · PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement
GlobalProtect successful login from CVE-2026-0257 pre-PoC exploitation IPs AIExploitation Medium
Hunts successful GlobalProtect portal/gateway sessions sourced from the nine IPs Unit 42 attributed to pre-PoC (before 2026-05-29) exploitation of the PAN-OS authentication bypass CVE-2026-0257. Any successful tunnel from these origins is a high-confidence foothold indicator. Rationale: Uses the nine source IPs Unit 42 explicitly attributed to pre-PoC (pre-2026-05-29) exploitation of CVE-2026-0257 against GlobalProtect portals/gateways. A successful gateway-connected event from any of these IPs is a direct foothold indicator. Falcon/CloudWatch empty because GlobalProtect VPN logs do not ship to FDR or CloudWatch Logs natively. Cross-checked against: • https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/active-exploitation-of-pan-os-cve-2026-0257/

  • Penetration testers / red team using one of the listed VPS providers (Frantech/BuyVM/Cogent ranges) for authorised assessments
  • Tor-exit overlap (23.128.228.6 is historically Tor-adjacent) for a user with a sanctioned anonymisation policy
  • Threat-intel feed back-population replaying these IPs into syslog after the fact
  • Recycled cloud IP no longer owned by the original adversary that a legitimate roaming user now uses

GlobalProtect connection with PoC-spoofed host-ID, MAC, or device name AIExploitation High
Alerts on successful GlobalProtect gateway-connected events whose host-id or machine name matches the placeholder MACs (aa:bb:cc:dd:ee:ff, 00:11:22:33:44:55) or generic device names (WINDOWS-LAPTOP-001, DESKTOP-GP01, GP-CLIENT) hard-coded by the public PoC for CVE-2026-0257. These identifiers should not appear in any legitimate corporate VPN client. Rationale: Targets the exact five placeholder identifiers Unit 42 published: two patently-fake MACs (aa:bb:cc:dd:ee:ff is the canonical example MAC; 00:11:22:33:44:55 is a sequence-pattern placeholder) and three generic Windows device names baked into the PoC. Defender query is intentionally a corroboration baseline against DeviceInfo — if those device names never legitimately exist in the estate, any VPN log referencing them is definitively spoofed. Cross-checked against: • https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/active-exploitation-of-pan-os-cve-2026-0257/
ATT&CKT1078 T1556 T1133
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Lab/test VPN client built with a placeholder MAC for QA
  • Vendor demo box temporarily named DESKTOP-GP01 during a Palo Alto Networks proof-of-value
  • Out-of-the-box Windows image not yet renamed (default DESKTOP-* pattern) connecting from a remote contractor
  • Internal red team replaying the PoC for authorised validation after disclosure
  • Long-tail of legacy MDM-unenrolled machines whose hostnames match the GP-CLIENT generic placeholder
Suppression filter
| where SourceUserName !in~ ("redteam-svc","qa-vpntest","sales-eng-demo")
Response runbook
Step 1 — Triage: pull the full GlobalProtect connection record (src_ip, src_user, host_id, machine_name, assigned_ip, client_version). Cross-reference DeviceInfo / asset inventory: does any owned asset legitimately use this hostname or MAC? Check whether the src_user has connected from this combo before. Step 2 — Contain: force-logout the GP session, disable the user account, block the source IP at the perimeter ACL, and isolate the tunnel-assigned internal IP from sensitive segments. Step 3 — Hunt: search the last 30 days of GP logs for the same host-id/MAC/hostname across other usernames (credential reuse), and pivot on the tunnel-assigned IP for SMB/RDP/WinRM/AD enumeration on internal hosts.
DeviceInfo
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| summarize LastSeen = max(Timestamp), DeviceCount = dcount(DeviceId) by DeviceName
| where DeviceName in~ ("WINDOWS-LAPTOP-001","DESKTOP-GP01","GP-CLIENT")
| project DeviceName, LastSeen, DeviceCount

   or SourceHostName in~ ("WINDOWS-LAPTOP-001","DESKTOP-GP01","GP-CLIENT")
