Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

          ParentProcessName

title: Office Application Spawning Script Host
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d07
status: experimental
description: |

    cscript, mshta, rundll32, regsvr32 — classic macro / embedded-

    - attack.t1059

    - attack.execution

    category: process_creation
    product: windows

        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\winword.exe'
            - '\excel.exe'
            - '\powerpnt.exe'

            - '\mspub.exe'
            - '\visio.exe'

            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
    condition: selection_parent and selection_child
falsepositives:
    - Legitimate automation macros in trusted internal documents
level: high

OfficeActivity

| where Operation in~ ("MessageSent","ChatCreated","TeamsImpersonationDetected")

   or Recipients has_any ("IT","helpdesk")
| project TimeGenerated, UserId, Operation, Subject, Recipients,
          ClientIP, UserAgent
| order by TimeGenerated desc

   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"

| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

PHPSpreadsheet phar:/// three-slash wrapper in HTTP request (CVE-2026-45034) AIExploitation High
Hunts inbound HTTP requests carrying the patch-bypass form 'phar:///path/file.phar/inner' (three or more slashes after the scheme) used to evade PHPSpreadsheet's File::prohibitWrappers helper. The single-slash form 'phar://x/' is correctly rejected by the patch; the triple-slash variant is the live bypass that still reaches PHP's phar stream wrapper. Rationale: Targets the literal bypass fingerprint from the advisory: 'phar:///path/file.phar/inner' with three or more slashes after the scheme. The advisory explicitly contrasts the rejected single-slash 'phar://x/dummy.csv' (negative control) against the surviving triple-slash 'phar:///work/exploit.phar/dummy.csv' (positive control), so a regex of `phar:/{3,}` plus its URL-encoded form is the exact patch-bypass discriminator. Defender XDR has no native inbound HTTP request telemetry so defender_kql is left empty; the detection lives in web-server / WAF logs that ship to Sentinel, Splunk Web, or Datadog. Did not perform external web search — the GitHub Security Advisory is itself the authoritative source for this CVE.
ATT&CKT1190 T1059
Data sourcesWeb
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Authorised vulnerability scanners (Nessus, Acunetix, Qualys, Burp Suite Pro) probing for stream-wrapper / phar deserialization CVEs
  • Internal red-team / purple-team exercises replaying the GHSA-87m4-826x-3crx PoC against staging
  • Developer regression tests against patched releases verifying the negative control still hits
  • Security researcher traffic against public bug-bounty scope using phar:// payloads on unrelated apps
Suppression filter
// Suppress known scanner User-Agents in Sentinel
| where UserAgent !has_any ("Nessus", "Qualys", "Acunetix", "Nuclei", "Burp", "OWASP ZAP", "WhiteHat", "AppScan")
// Suppress allow-listed pentest source ranges (replace with your watchlist)
| where SourceIP !in ("203.0.113.0/24")
Response runbook
Step 1 (triage): Confirm the target endpoint hosts a PHPSpreadsheet-backed feature (file upload, XLSX/CSV import, report generator). Cross-check the source IP against scanner allow-lists; pull the full request body if WAF retains it and identify the 'file' / 'filename' / 'path' parameter that carried the phar:/// value. Capture the User-Agent and any session/auth context (was this authenticated?). Step 2 (contain): Block the source IP at the WAF/edge; if the target is PHP 7.x with PHPSpreadsheet <= 1.30.4, treat as confirmed RCE and isolate the web host from internal networks; snapshot for forensics. Apply the str_contains('://') fix or upgrade to the fixed PHPSpreadsheet tag. Step 3 (hunt): Pivot on the host running UC2 (PHP-FPM / Apache spawning shell or network tools within 60s of the request), UC3 (.phar files written by the web-server user to upload/temp dirs), outbound DNS/HTTP from the web process to non-CDN destinations, and new files in /tmp, /dev/shm, web-root cron, or .ssh/a
let bypass_pattern = @"(?i)phar:/{3,}|phar%3a%2f%2f%2f";
let wrapper_pattern = @"(?i)phar:/{2}|phar%3a%2f%2f";
(W3CIISLog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| extend FullUrl = strcat(csUriStem, "?", csUriQuery)
| where FullUrl matches regex wrapper_pattern
| extend IsBypassVariant = iff(FullUrl matches regex bypass_pattern, "yes", "no")
| project TimeGenerated, SourceIP = cIP, URL = FullUrl, UserAgent = csUserAgent, Method = csMethod, Status = scStatus, IsBypassVariant, LogSource = "W3CIISLog")
| union (CommonSecurityLog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where RequestURL matches regex wrapper_pattern
| extend IsBypassVariant = iff(RequestURL matches regex bypass_pattern, "yes", "no")
| project TimeGenerated, SourceIP, URL = RequestURL, UserAgent = RequestClientApplication, Method = RequestMethod, Status = tostring(EventOutcome), IsBypassVariant, LogSource = "CommonSecurityLog")
| where IsBypassVariant == "yes"   // 3+ slashes after scheme = patch bypass
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Web-server process (php-fpm / apache / nginx / w3wp) spawning shell or network tooling AIExploitation Medium
Hunts post-exploitation behaviour following CVE-2026-45034 RCE on PHP 7.x — the phar metadata __wakeup/__destruct gadget runs inside the web-server worker, so the parent of any spawned shell/network LOLBin is php-fpm, apache2, httpd, nginx, or w3wp.exe. Article-specific because PHP web workers almost never legitimately spawn sh/bash/curl/wget/nc. Rationale: The advisory states the gadget chain runs '__wakeup' and '__destruct' on PHP 7.x 'inside the victim process' — meaning the PHP web worker. So the post-RCE pivot is the same web-worker process spawning anything that's not another PHP worker. Excluding php→php parents (legitimate FPM pool spawning) and constraining child to shell/network LOLBins narrows the FP surface. Marked hunting (not alerting) because legitimate apps do shell out from PHP for image/video processing. Did not perform external web search — the technical detail in the GitHub advisory was sufficient to write this detection.
ATT&CKT1059.004 T1059.001 T1190 T1505.003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • WordPress / Drupal plugins that legitimately exec ImageMagick (convert), ffmpeg, or pdftotext via shell
  • PHP-based CI/CD or deployment dashboards that shell out to git / rsync / ssh
  • Composer post-install hooks running in production with shell child processes
  • Monitoring / health-check PHP endpoints that exec system commands (df, uptime, ps)
  • Backup scripts triggered from web cron that invoke curl / wget to S3 / cloud storage
Suppression filter
// Defender — drop known-good wrappers
| where ProcessCommandLine !has_any ("/usr/bin/convert", "ImageMagick", "ffmpeg", "pdftotext", "/opt/app/healthcheck.sh")
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine !has "composer"
Response runbook
Step 1 (triage): Pull the parent PHP worker's recent request log (Apache/Nginx access log within 5 minutes of the child spawn) and look for 'phar://' or 'phar%3A%2F%2F' in any URL or POST body — that's the smoking gun tying this to UC1. Check the spawned child's cmdline for download URLs, reverse-shell signatures, or base64 blobs. Confirm whether the host runs PHP 7.x and PHPSpreadsheet <= 1.30.4 (1.x branch). Step 2 (contain): Kill the spawned child + parent worker, isolate the web host from internal networks, snapshot for forensics. Block any outbound IPs/domains the child contacted. Step 3 (hunt): Look for newly-written files in /tmp, /dev/shm, /var/tmp, web-root upload dirs, and ~/.ssh/. Pivot on the web user's account for new cron entries, systemd timers, or .bashrc modifications. Search the asset for .phar files (UC3) and check WAF for other phar:/// requests across the estate.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("php-fpm","php-fpm.exe","php","php.exe","php-cgi.exe","apache2","httpd","httpd.exe","nginx","nginx.exe","w3wp.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("sh","bash","dash","zsh","ksh","cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","curl","curl.exe","wget","wget.exe","nc","ncat","ncat.exe","socat","python","python3","python.exe","perl","ruby","certutil.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| where AccountName != "" and AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          ParentCmd   = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildImage  = FolderPath,
          ChildCmd    = ProcessCommandLine,
          SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("php-fpm","php.exe","php","php-cgi.exe","apache2","httpd","nginx","w3wp.exe")
| where ActingProcessName has_any ("sh","bash","dash","zsh","cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","curl","wget","nc","ncat","socat","python","python3","perl","ruby","certutil.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, Hash
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Phar archive or PHPSpreadsheet RCE marker written by web-server process AIDelivery Medium
Hunts for .phar files written into web-server-writable directories by a PHP/web-worker process (staging step of CVE-2026-45034) and for the PoC artefact file 'pwned_marker' that the published exploit drops on successful RCE. .phar uploads to web roots are extremely unusual outside of deliberate deployment. Rationale: Two article-specific strings drive this: '.phar' (the exploit archive extension referenced throughout the advisory — exploit.phar in the PoC) and 'pwned_marker' (the literal file the published gadget writes containing 'WAKEUP: phpspreadsheet-bypass' / 'DESTRUCT: phpspreadsheet-bypass'). Both must be written by a web-worker process, which is the parent constraint that turns it from a generic 'phar file appeared' dashboard into a CVE-tied detection. Did not perform external web search — the advisory contained sufficient detail.
ATT&CKT1505.003 T1190 T1105
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Legitimate Composer / Phar deployment where a build step writes packaged .phar archives into the webroot (rare in production but happens in dev environments)
  • Security team / internal red-team running the GHSA-87m4-826x-3crx run.sh reproducer against a lab
  • Static-analysis tooling that materialises phar test fixtures during CI runs that share a host with a webserver
  • Backup or update scripts that include phar files among the artefacts they restore into the web root
Suppression filter
// Defender — exclude known build / deploy paths
| where FolderPath !has_any ("/opt/build/", "/var/cache/composer/", "/tmp/composer-", "\\build\\artifacts\\")
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine !has "composer"
Response runbook
Step 1 (triage): Hash the written file (SHA256) and check against threat-intel; correlate timestamp with the webserver access log to find the request that triggered the write — look specifically for 'phar://' or the IOFactory::load entry point in the application logs. Read the file content if 'pwned_marker' was found (the published PoC writes 'WAKEUP: phpspreadsheet-bypass' and 'DESTRUCT: phpspreadsheet-bypass'). Step 2 (contain): Quarantine the file, isolate the host, identify whether the asset is PHP 7.x with PHPSpreadsheet <= 1.30.4 (the only branch with RCE exposure). Reset any credentials that were resident in the web process environment. Step 3 (hunt): Look for other .phar artefacts across the estate written by web workers in the last 30 days; pivot on the writing-process command line for IOFactory::load patterns; check for the post-RCE shell-spawn (UC2) on the same host within ±5 minutes; review WAF for inbound 'phar://' requests (UC1) targeting the same endpoint.
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("php-fpm","php-fpm.exe","php","php.exe","php-cgi.exe","apache2","httpd","httpd.exe","nginx","nginx.exe","w3wp.exe")
| where FileName endswith ".phar"
     or FileName =~ "pwned_marker"
     or FileName =~ "exploit.phar"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, FileSize,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventType in ("FileCreated", "FileModified")
| where ActingProcessName has_any ("php-fpm","php.exe","php","php-cgi","apache2","httpd","nginx","w3wp.exe")
| where TargetFileName endswith ".phar"
     or TargetFileName =~ "pwned_marker"
     or TargetFileName =~ "exploit.phar"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, TargetFileSize, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetFileSHA256
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Phar Archive or pwned_marker File Written by PHP / Web-Server Worker (CVE-2026-45034)
id: 9b3d8c1e-4f6a-4c7b-8e2d-5a9c1f7b3d6e
description: Detects .phar archives or the published PoC 'pwned_marker' file being written by a php-fpm, apache, nginx, or w3wp worker — both are staging or successful-exploit artefacts for CVE-2026-45034.
references:
  - https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-87m4-826x-3crx
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/06/09
tags:
  - attack.initial_access
  - attack.t1190
  - attack.persistence
  - attack.t1505.003
logsource:
  category: file_event
detection:
  parent_webworker:
    Image|endswith:
      - '/php-fpm'
      - '/php'
      - '/php-cgi'
      - '/apache2'
      - '/httpd'
      - '/nginx'
      - '\php-fpm.exe'
      - '\php.exe'
      - '\httpd.exe'
      - '\nginx.exe'
      - '\w3wp.exe'
  phar_artefact:
    TargetFilename|endswith: '.phar'
  poc_marker:
    TargetFilename|endswith:
      - '/pwned_marker'
      - '\pwned_marker'
      - '/exploit.phar'
      - '\exploit.phar'
  condition: parent_webworker and (phar_artefact or poc_marker)
falsepositives:
  - Deliberate phar-based application deployment / composer package extraction
  - Internal red-team replaying the published PoC against a lab host
level: high
(source:linux.auditd OR source:windows.sysmon) AND @process.name:(*php-fpm* OR php OR *php.exe OR *apache2* OR *httpd* OR *nginx* OR *w3wp.exe*) AND (@file.name:*.phar OR @file.name:pwned_marker OR @file.name:exploit.phar OR @file.path:*pwned_marker* OR @file.path:*\.phar)
#event_simpleName=FileWritten

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("52fda5c1b9704544f32ee98d9060e689", "51d39aa39478beeac94f2d12f682ecce") or SHA1 in~ ("52fda5c1b9704544f32ee98d9060e689", "51d39aa39478beeac94f2d12f682ecce") or MD5 in~ ("52fda5c1b9704544f32ee98d9060e689", "51d39aa39478beeac94f2d12f682ecce")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("52fda5c1b9704544f32ee98d9060e689", "51d39aa39478beeac94f2d12f682ecce") or SHA1 in~ ("52fda5c1b9704544f32ee98d9060e689", "51d39aa39478beeac94f2d12f682ecce") or MD5 in~ ("52fda5c1b9704544f32ee98d9060e689", "51d39aa39478beeac94f2d12f682ecce")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["52fda5c1b9704544f32ee98d9060e689", "51d39aa39478beeac94f2d12f682ecce"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Shopper: Authorization bypass and RBAC privilege escalation in team settings

CVEsCVE-2026-47744

Actions on Objectives
—
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] HTTP access to Shopper admin team-settings / Livewire endpoints (CVE-2026-47744)
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
HTTP access to Shopper admin team-settings / Livewire endpoints (CVE-2026-47744) AIExploitation Medium
Hunts HTTP requests to the vulnerable Shopper admin panel routes — Settings/Team/Index and Settings/Team/RolePermission — including their Livewire AJAX endpoints. Pre-patch (Shopper < 2.8.0) these were reachable by any authenticated panel user and were the only network observable for the authorization-bypass / RBAC privilege-escalation chain disclosed in GHSA-c3qp-2ggw-xjg7. Rationale: The article names exactly two Livewire components — Settings/Team/Index and Settings/Team/RolePermission — as the abuse surface. Livewire routes those component names verbatim through /livewire/update (Livewire v3) and /livewire/message/{component} (v2), so the component string and these endpoints are the highest-fidelity strings available pre-exploit. POST/PUT/PATCH on the role-permission route is the specific action that grants/escalates permissions, distinguishing exploitation from normal page loads. No external IOC corroboration was attempted since the advisory exposes no network IOCs — only source-code paths.
ATT&CKT1190 T1068 T1078.003
Data sourcesWeb.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Legitimate Shopper panel administrators (manage_users holders) creating roles or updating role permissions in normal operations
  • Internal QA / penetration-test exercises against the upgraded (>=2.8.0) panel
  • Application monitoring/uptime probes hitting the admin panel index without authentication (will 302 to login but still appear in HTTP logs)
  • Crawlers / bots probing /livewire/update on any Livewire-built site (broad Livewire scanners)
  • Reverse-proxy retries duplicating the same POST to /livewire/update on transient 5xx
Suppression filter
| where not (User in~ ("admin","superadmin") and ClientIP in ("10.0.0.0/8","192.168.0.0/16")) and not (UserAgent has_any ("Pingdom","UptimeRobot","StatusCake"))
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the Shopper composer.lock from the affected host and confirm shopper/framework < 2.8.0; if patched, downgrade severity. Identify the authenticated session behind the request (panel session cookie or X-Forwarded-User) and map to the panel user — was it a holder of view_users only? 2) Contain: force-logout all panel sessions, rotate panel admin credentials, and either upgrade to shopper/framework 2.8.0 immediately or block /livewire/update and /admin/settings/team* at the WAF until upgrade. 3) Hunt: dump the roles and role_permission tables and diff against a known-good snapshot — look for newly granted manage_users / edit_orders to non-admin accounts; review the panel users table for unexpected deletions of legitimate administrators; pivot on the source IP across the prior 30 days for any other panel reconnaissance.
// Requires IIS / nginx access logs ingested to a custom table; M365 Defender XDR does not natively capture inbound HTTP to a self-hosted PHP app.
// Pivot via DeviceNetworkEvents for the web server's inbound traffic to the panel's listening port, then triage in the proxy/WAF.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where LocalPort in (80, 443, 8080, 8443)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("w3wp.exe","php-fpm.exe","php.exe","nginx.exe","httpd.exe","caddy.exe")
| where ActionType == "InboundConnectionAccepted"
| summarize Connections = count(), FirstSeen = min(Timestamp), LastSeen = max(Timestamp) by DeviceName, LocalIP, LocalPort, RemoteIP, InitiatingProcessFileName
| where Connections > 5
| order by LastSeen desc
let TargetPaths = dynamic(["settings/team","settings.team.index","settings.team.role-permission","settings.team.rolepermission","/livewire/update","/livewire/message/settings.team"]);
(union isfuzzy=true
    (W3CIISLog
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where csUriStem has_any (TargetPaths) or csUriQuery has_any (TargetPaths)
        | project TimeGenerated, Source="IIS", Computer, Method=csMethod, Url=strcat(csUriStem, iff(isnotempty(csUriQuery), strcat("?", csUriQuery), "")), Status=scStatus, ClientIP=cIP, UserAgent=csUserAgent, User=csUserName),
    (AppServiceHTTPLogs
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where CsUriStem has_any (TargetPaths) or CsUriQuery has_any (TargetPaths)
        | project TimeGenerated, Source="AppService", Computer=CsHost, Method=CsMethod, Url=strcat(CsUriStem, iff(isnotempty(CsUriQuery), strcat("?", CsUriQuery), "")), Status=ScStatus, ClientIP=CIp, UserAgent=UserAgent, User=CsUsername),
    (CommonSecurityLog
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where RequestURL has_any (TargetPaths)
        | project TimeGenerated, Source=DeviceProduct, Computer=DestinationHostName, Method=RequestMethod, Url=RequestURL, Status=tostring(EventOutcome), ClientIP=SourceIP, UserAgent=RequestClientApplication, User=SourceUserName)
)
| where Method in~ ("POST","PUT","PATCH") or Url has_any ("role-permission","rolepermission")
| summarize Hits = count(), FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen = max(TimeGenerated), Methods = make_set(Method), Statuses = make_set(Status), UserAgents = make_set(UserAgent, 5), Users = make_set(User, 10) by ClientIP, Computer, Source
| order by LastSeen desc
title: Shopper Admin Team Settings / Livewire Endpoint Access (CVE-2026-47744)
id: 9f6c5d22-2e4f-4b3a-9d6e-7c8a2b9d4e10
status: experimental
description: Detects HTTP requests to Shopper admin panel team-settings routes (Settings/Team/Index, Settings/Team/RolePermission) and their underlying Livewire endpoints, which were the abuse path for CVE-2026-47744 (authorization bypass + RBAC privilege escalation) in shopper/framework versions below 2.8.0.
references:
    - https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-c3qp-2ggw-xjg7
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/06/05
tags:
    - attack.t1190
    - attack.t1068
    - attack.t1078.003
    - cve.2026.47744
logsource:
    category: webserver
detection:
    selection_path:
        cs-uri-stem|contains:
            - 'settings/team'
            - 'settings.team.index'
            - 'settings.team.role-permission'
            - 'settings.team.rolepermission'
            - '/livewire/update'
            - '/livewire/message/settings.team'
    condition: selection_path
falsepositives:
    - Legitimate Shopper administrators managing roles in the admin panel
    - Internal QA/staging exercising the team settings page
level: medium
(source:nginx OR source:apache OR source:iis OR source:azure.app_services) (@http.url_details.path:(*settings/team* OR *settings.team.index* OR *settings.team.role-permission* OR *settings.team.rolePermission*) OR @http.url_details.path:(*/livewire/update* OR */livewire/message/settings.team*)) AND @http.method:(POST OR PUT OR PATCH)
# log group: /aws/applicationelb (or /aws/cloudfront/access-logs, /aws/wafv2) — point at whichever fronts the Shopper admin panel
fields @timestamp, request_url, request_verb, elb_status_code, client_ip, user_agent, target_processing_time
| filter (request_url like /settings\/team/ 
         or request_url like /settings\.team\.index/ 
         or request_url like /settings\.team\.role-?permission/i
         or request_url like /\/livewire\/(update|message\/settings\.team)/)
      and request_verb in ["POST","PUT","PATCH"]
| sort @timestamp desc
| limit 200

Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-47744")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-47744")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc
